美期中選舉牽動市場表現，股債配置重品質、控風險
2026 年 11 月 04 日
一、 市場動態
1. 美期中選舉牽動市場情緒，亞股一度隨美股期貨下挫：美國 11 月 3 日舉行期中選舉
，根據 CNN 網站的結果顯示，截至台北時間 11/4 下午 3 點左右，民主黨於參議院
已取得 100 席中的 53 席的過半席次，於眾議院則在 435 席中取得了 225 席(218 席
過半)。市場重新評估國會控制權及川普政府未來政策推動能力，擔心民主黨國會影響
力擴大，可能對財政政策及科技企業監管形成新的制衡。S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100

期貨在開票期間一度下挫 1.38%及 2.35%，科技股明顯承受較大賣壓。惟隨後跌深反
彈，截至台北時間下午 3 時左右，S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100 期貨跌幅已收斂至
0.22%及 0.44%。亞洲市場方面，科技股為主及出口導向股市受到美股期貨拖累，台
灣加權指數 11/4 盤中最低下挫 3.38%，所幸隨跌幅收斂，終場下跌 0.83%；韓股同
日也由大跌近 4%由黑翻紅上漲 0.62%。11/3 未開盤的日股，在 11/4 開盤後出現補
跌，最低下挫近 4.8%，但最後跌幅收斂至下跌 1.42%。
2. 美國公債及美元同步反映政策預期調整，市場避險情緒升溫：美國公債及美元反映政
策預期調整，殖利率盤中先跌後漲：10 年期公債殖利率盤中先跌後漲，截至台北時間
下午 3 時來到 4.906%；同一時間，美元指數小跌約 0.37%。股、債、匯市的變動顯
示，投資人除了調整股票風險部位，也在評估未來美國財政刺激規模、政府債務供給
與利率前景。
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨取得國會控制權，市場重新定價財政與監管政策：本次選舉對金融市場的主要

影響，在於未來行政與立法部門的權力分配可能改變。共和黨先前同時掌握白宮與國
會，有利於推動稅制、財政及產業政策；民主黨在參眾兩院均取得過半席次後，部分

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
政策將面臨更高的協商成本。財政方面，新增減稅及擴大支出的空間可能受到限制，
市場因而下修部分財政刺激預期；企業方面，國會監督權可能增加對大型科技企業、
AI 資料中心、反壟斷及資料隱私議題的調查壓力。惟國會控制權改變不代表既有政策
立即逆轉，也不代表所有監管措施均能完成立法。此次市場反應更可能是投資人重新
要求政策風險溢酬，而非企業獲利已發生實質下修。
2. AI 資本支出與投資回報疑慮尚未消退，放大科技股對政治事件的反應：此次選舉開票
期間，Nasdaq 期貨及與科技相關的亞股出現大幅波動，亦與近期科技股基本面及評

價爭議有關。10 月底以來美股因大型科技企業 AI 投資效益受到質疑而大幅修正，市
場關注焦點逐漸由 AI 基礎建設支出規模，轉向相關投資能否創造足夠的營運現金流與
股東報酬。以雲端服務及資料中心產業而言，企業雖可透過 AI 運算需求增加帶動營收
成長，但 GPU、伺服器、電力設備及資料中心建設需要龐大前期資金，若營收回收速
度不如預期，可能使自由現金流承壓並增加外部融資需求。近期部分科技企業籌資計
畫及財測不佳，已使投資人提高對財務品質的要求。當市場原先已對 AI 投資回報存有
疑慮，新的政治及監管不確定性便更容易放大評價修正幅度。
三、 市場看法
1. 半導體需求仍具分化，政治事件不等於 AI 產業成長趨勢反轉：科技股近期的股價修正
與 AI 終端需求惡化並非相同概念。AMD 近期公布優於市場預期的財報，並對資料中
心業務提出較強勁的成長展望，顯示 AI 運算需求仍具有一定韌性；相對而言，Intel
先前財測疲弱及現金流壓力，則反映不同半導體企業的產品競爭力、資本效率及終端
市場曝險存在明顯差異。因此，不宜將半導體股整體下跌解讀為 AI 需求全面降溫。從

產業鏈觀察，具備技術領先優勢、穩定客戶需求及良好現金流的企業，與高度依賴外
部融資、尚未形成穩定獲利模式的基礎建設業者，對資金成本及監管變化的承受能力
並不相同。未來市場可能更加重視個別企業的獲利品質，而非單純以 AI 相關營收成長
作為估值依據。
2. 政治事件可能延長科技股評價調整，但中期走勢仍取決於企業獲利：本次期中選舉引
發的期貨跌勢，反映市場對政策方向及國會控制權變化的即時反應，惟政治不確定性
通常須透過實際立法、企業投資計畫或經濟活動變化，才會形成較持久的基本面影響
。從企業獲利角度觀察，路透社引用 LSEG 資料指出，S&P 500 第三季企業獲利仍呈
現強勁成長，科技企業對整體獲利貢獻顯著，顯示目前尚無充分證據支持美國企業基
本面全面轉弱。不過，科技股先前已累積較高成長預期，市場對資本支出回報及監管
風險的容忍度降低，可能使股價即使在營收成長下仍承受評價壓力。短期而言，若選
舉結果逐漸明朗，且企業獲利預估未進一步下修，市場有機會逐步消化政治風險；反
之，若政策不確定性伴隨獲利預期下修，修正時間可能延長。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：根據彭博統計，1950
年以來美國歷次期中選舉後 12 個月，S&P 500 平均報酬率約 15%，且 19 次全數
收正。其中最高為 1954 年的逾 30%，表現最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。我們認為
，選前政策不確定性消除加上府會分治抑制財政與監管的轉向是為歷史上選後行情的
主要支撐，但相關數據所呈現的為過往經驗，未來股市表現能否延續先前情況仍應觀
察後續基本面變化。
4. 美國公債具避險價值，但利率下行能否持續仍取決於通膨與財政前景：在美國期中選

舉開票期間，美股期貨下跌伴隨美債殖利率回落，顯示公債仍能在股市波動期間提供
一定的風險分散效果。然而，本次殖利率下降可能同時包含避險買盤、財政刺激預期
下修及市場對經濟成長前景重新評估等因素，不宜直接解讀為聯準會即將轉向寬鬆。
尤其能源市場仍受到中東地緣政治及供應中斷風險影響，油價波動可能使通膨前景反
覆，限制長天期公債殖利率的下行空間。若未來財政擴張受到限制，可能對長端利率
形成部分支撐；但若政治僵局提高債務上限協商風險，亦可能使美債期限溢酬波動。
因此，債券配置仍須同時考量收益率、存續期間與通膨風險，不宜僅根據單日行情決
定配置方向。
5. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：目前科技股修正主
要反映評價與政策風險，但若 AI 企業持續增加資本支出及外部融資，市場對債務負擔
與償債能力的關注可能進一步升高。對大型投資等級科技企業而言，較穩定的營運現
金流及融資管道有助於吸收資本支出壓力；但對依賴單一客戶合約、設備抵押融資或
高槓桿經營的 AI 基礎建設業者，利率與信用利差上升可能更直接影響投資回報及再融

資能力。後續應追蹤投資等級債與高收益債 OAS、科技及雲端相關發行人利差、新債
發行需求及信用評等展望。若科技股下跌但整體信用利差維持穩定，市場風險可能仍
以股權評價調整為主；若信用利差同步持續擴大，則須提高對金融條件收緊及風險跨
市場擴散的警覺。

四、 投資建議：
1. 科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，惟短線宜審慎布局並重視個股分化：美國期中選舉帶
來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，惟大型雲端業者持續投入 AI 基礎建
設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，目前尚不宜因美股期貨在開票期間的
下挫即全面轉向保守。我們仍建議維持美股核心配置，但短期避免過度集中於高評價
、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企業。產業方面，資訊科技維持選擇性配置，優
先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業；對雲端基
礎建設及 AI 融資相關標的，則應提高財務結構與現金流審查標準。

2. 維持中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債配置，兼顧收益與風險分散：在美國政策風
險升溫及科技股評價調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源
價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延長存續期間。建議以中天期美國公債及高信評投資
等級債作為主要配置方向，兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置穩定性。信用債方
面，建議優先選擇營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人，對 AI 相關
企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

Morgan Stanley Research 3
A Closer Look at the Fundamentals
Why we changed our minds – March caution, May upside
surprise
We downgraded Winbond and Nanya Tech in March (Old Memory: Turning Selective) as
elevated DDR4 pricing encouraged supply responses and customer pushback, pointing to
narrower shortages and moderating price hikes. In May, we reversed those calls as lower
expected supply from Micron and Hynix, alongside stronger server and enterprise SSD

demand, widened the projected supply-demand gap (Old Memory: Upside Surprise
Ahead). The distinction matters: March was a selective DDR4 call; today, we see a broader
challenge to old memory’s pricing durability.
What has changed
Pricing power cannot offset weaker demand indefinitely
Our constructive thesis was that niche memory, with limited fixed-price LTA constraints,
could reprice faster and outperform mainstream memory. We now see that flexibility

becoming a two-way risk. Rising uncertainty over cloud spending weakens confidence in
incremental enterprise demand, while much weaker consumer demand challenges
customers’ ability to absorb further hikes.
After the US midterms, our US team now expects ~20% of 2028e US
data center capacity additions to shift to 2029
This include some projects in Texas and Ohio, as governors introduce new data center
legislation (refer to Buy the Drop: AI Investment Opportunities Post the US Midterms by

Stephen Byrd). Stephen has shifted ~5GW of US data center capacity additions from 2028
to 2029  ( Exhibit 11 ) – equivalent to 20% of US and 10% of global 2028 capacity
additions. This implies a 4% Y/Y decline in US data center GW additions  in 2028 before a
38% Y/Y rise in 2029. We now expect global data center additions to increase 9% in 2028
and    20% Y/Y  in 2029.
This could create an air pocket across the tech supply chain in 2028,
potentially reducing pricing power and leading to an inventory build

Our latest PC semis work highlights procurement pull-ins in 1Q–3Q26, followed by a
deeper correction in 4Q, reinforcing the risk that earlier orders overstated underlying
consumption (PC Semis: More Divergent Outlooks).
Supply-demand update –  looking beyond the shortage
Our revised models for SLC NAND, MLC NAND, NOR, and DDR4 point to progressively
more balanced markets, particularly into 2028. We now expect 2028 supply-demand
deficits of -3%, -5%, and -1% for SLC NAND, MLC NAND, NOR, with a +1% excess for DDR4.

Near-term tightness need not disappear immediately for stocks to de-rate: a narrowing
shortage can reduce incremental pricing power before earnings peak. We therefore place

Morgan Stanley Research 11
Winbond: Estimate Revisions and Quarterly Financials
We adjust our EPS estimates +8% for 2026, -9% for 2027, and -22% for 2028:  The
2026 EPS increase reflects 3Q26 actual results. We also factor in a potential peak in
pricing power across NOR, SLC, and DDR4. Compared to the previous estimate, we now
reduce our flash wafer ASP assumptions throughout 2027-2028 and  26% into 4Q28, and
reduce our DRAM ASP per bit estimates throughout 2027-2028 and  23% into 4Q28,

without changing our flash or DRAM shipment estimates. Apart from reduced revenue
estimates, we also lower our gross margin estimates to account for the reduced ASP
estimates. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates are largely a function of changes in
our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is a good time to make these
changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating price hikes, and
potentially delays in 2028 US cloud capex.

Winbond: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to  NT$120
We continue to apply P/B multiple methodology to derive our price target, in line with our
approach for Greater China memory IDM peers and in view of the industry's high earnings
volatility. Our price target (base case scenario value) is set at 1.2x 2027e BVPS vs. our
prior target multiple of 2.9x 2027e BVPS, to reflect the potential peak in pricing power.
Our 1.2x target P/B is above our 1.0x P/B valuation anchor –  we still favor the company's

CUBE and S-SiCap businesses for their long-term potential. We see 1.0x P/B as the
downside valuation anchor because book value provides a psychological support level for
capital-intensive IDM/foundry stocks during cyclical downturns. When earnings are
temporarily depressed, investors typically place greater weight on the underlying net
asset base.
Our base case value/price target cut of 58% is almost entirely a function  of reduced target
multiple, rather than change in 2027e BVPS, which declines only 2% in our new estimate.

Our bull case value is reduced from NT$342 to NT$180, and our bear case value is
reduced from NT$118 to NT$50, based on 1.8x and 0.5x 2027e BVPS, respectively
(previously 3.4x and 1.2x).
We downgrade Winbond to EW
We continue to like the CUBE and S-SiCap businesses for longer-term potential, but we
see near-term pressure from NOR, SLC NAND, and DDR4 businesses, in all of which
industry supply is ramping faster than demand is increasing, especially as global cloud

capex may face partial delay from 2028 to 2029. We believe the stock still has strong
valuation support at 1.0-1.2x P/B, and therefore we believe an EW rating is fair.

GigaDevice: Estimate Revisions and Quarterly Financials
We cut our EPS estimates  25% for 2026, 36% for 2027, and 33% for 2028:  The 2026e
EPS change mainly reflects actual 2Q26 and 3Q26 results. The 2027e and 2028e EPS
changes reflect a potential peak in pricing power across DDR4, NOR Flash, and SLC NAND.
We now reduce our DRAM and others revenue estimates throughout 2027-2028 and 43%
into 4Q28, mainly reflecting changes in our DDR4 ASP estimates. We also reduce our

Flash (including NOR and SLC NAND) ASP estimates throughout 2027-2028 and  25% into
4Q28, without changing our Flash shipment estimates. We did not materially change our
GM assumptions. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates are largely a function of
changes in our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is a good time to make
these changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating price hikes, and
potential delay in 2028 US cloud capex.

GigaDevice: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target  to Rmb259
We continue to derive our price target, which is our base case value, from a residual
income model. We believe this best captures the stock’s long-term value.
Our base case value cut of 65% can be attributed ~33% to earnings estimate revisions, as
has been elaborated, and ~33% from multiple compression, driven by intermediate growth
rate cut and beta increase.  (Note: the overall price target change is calculated from the

fully revised RI model. The individual contribution estimates are illustrative and should
not be added together or compounded to derive the overall change.).
We cut our intermediate growth rate to 8.0% from 11.3% to reflect stronger changes in
the supply environment compared to the end market demand over the medium to long
term. The potential peak in pricing power for niche memory (DDR4, SLC, MLC, NOR) and
the potential delay of 2028 US cloud capex now suggest slower revenue growth for

GigaDevice from 2026 to 2036. We think a 3.3ppt p.a. growth rate cut reflects the new
supply/demand dynamics with a closer gap.
We also raise our beta to 1.50 from 1.14, leading our cost of equity assumption to rise to
11.8% from 9.4%. This  reflects a cautious valuation scenario, to account for the recent
memory segment volatility, 70%+ correction from the stock price peak in June, and the
fabless model, which does not provide a floor support for valuation similar to the IDM

model. The elevated beta reflects the company's exposure to rapidly changing niche
memory pricing dynamics that could severely affect earnings with a downward bias
compared to our financial forecasts.
Our other key assumptions remain unchanged, including equity risk premium of 6.5%, risk-
free rate of 2% and payout ratio of 40%.
Our bull case value falls to Rmb448 from Rmb1,300, and our bear case value to Rmb112
from Rmb324.
We downgrade GigaDevice to EW

We still see the company as a domestic leader in MCU localization and believe its
customized DRAM business could show a revenue ramp over the next 1-2 years. We also
still like its strategic partnership with CXMT, which provides both capacity support and
process tech improvements. However, we see near-term pressure from the NOR, SLC
NAND, and DDR4 businesses, in all of which industry supply is ramping faster than
demand increase, especially as global cloud capex may face partial delay from 2028 to

2029. Fabless design houses do not face depreciation and utilization issues during a down-
cycle, but at the same time they lack a similar valuation anchor as IDM/foundry players
from their book value, and therefore we see potentially continued high volatility ahead.
Our new price target implies a 2027e P/E of 9x, below the historical average of 38x since
2016, owing to changes in pricing power.

PSMC: Earnings Estimate     Revisions
We    adjust our EPS forecasts +10% for 2026 and -11% each for 2027 and 2028:      The
2026e EPS change mainly reflects actual 2Q26 and 3Q26 results. The 2027e and 2028e
EPS changes reflect a potential peak in pricing power across DDR3/3 and SLC NAND. We
now reduce our DRAM wafer ASP estimates throughout 2027-2028 and 19% into 4Q28,
without changing our DRAM wafer shipment estimates. We did not materially change our

GM assumptions. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates are largely a function of
changes in our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is a good time to make
these changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating price hikes, and
potential delay in 2028 US cloud capex.

PSMC: Valuation     Methodology
Our price target  is cut to NT$54
We incorporate our revised estimates and maintain our P/B valuation methodology. Our
new target, which is our base case value,  is based on a 2027e P/B of 1.0x, down from 2.0x
previously –  below the historical average of 1.5x since 2021, to reflect pricing
uncertainties.
Our base case value/price target cut of 51% is almost entirely a result of target multiple
reduction, rather than change in 2027e BVPS, which declined only 1.5% in our new

estimate. For PSMC, we use 1.0x P/B as the downside valuation anchor –  book value
provides a psychological support level for capital-intensive IDM/foundry stocks during
cyclical downturns. When earnings are temporarily depressed, investors typically place
greater weight on the underlying net asset base.
We decrease our bull case value from NT$177 to NT$86 and our bear case value from NT
$55 to NT$27, implying 1.6x and 0.5x 2027e P/B, respectively.

We downgrade PSMC to EW
We are positive on the company's emerging 3D AI foundry business, and we believe PSMC
is well positioned to benefit from its Micron partnership, including front-end migration to
the one-year  process node and back-end opportunities in advanced DRAM production
(e.g., HBM). The company is also catching up with the DRAM pricing up-cycle.
At the same time, its logic foundry business is set to enjoy continued price hikes into 2027.

However, on its memory foundry side, we see near-term pressure from DDR3/4, in which
industry supply is ramping faster than demand is increasing, especially as global cloud
capex may face partial delay from 2028 to 2029.
We believe the stock still has strong valuation support at 1.0x P/B, and therefore we
believe an EW rating is fair.

Macronix: Earnings  Estimate Revisions
We cut our 2026, 2027, and 2028 EPS forecasts 14%, 20%, and 21%, respectively: The
2026e EPS change mainly reflects actual 3Q26 results. The 2027e and 2028e EPS
changes reflect a potential peak in pricing power across NOR and MLC/SLC NAND. We
now reduce our NAND and /NOR wafer ASP estimates throughout 2027-2028 and 15%
and 8% into 4Q28 respectively, without changing our wafer shipment estimates. We did

not materially change our GM assumptions. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates are
largely a function of changes in our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is a
good time to make these changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating
price hikes, and potential delay in 2028 US cloud capex.

Macronix: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to NT$90 from NT$220
We continue to use a P/B multiple methodology to derive our price target, which is our
base case value, in line with our approach for Greater China memory IDM peers and in
view of the industry's high volatility. Our new target is based on a 2027e P/B of 1.0x, down
from 2.1x previously – below the average of 1.5x since 2017, given the potential peak in
pricing power for NOR and SLC/MLC NAND. For Macronix, we now use 1.0x P/B as the

downside valuation anchor because book value provides a psychological support level for
capital-intensive IDM/foundry stocks during cyclical downturns. When earnings are
temporarily depressed, investors typically place greater weight on the underlying net
asset base.
Our base case value/price target cut of 59% reflects both target multiple reduction and a
15% 2027 BVPS estimate reduction (relatively large because of a smaller base).
We cut our bull case value from NT$355 to NT$145, which is 1.6x our 2027e BVPS. We cut

our bear case value from NT$100 to NT$41, which is 0.5x our 2027e BVPS.
We downgrade Macronix to EW
We continue to like its unique position in MLC NAND as the only major supplier still
ramping up capacity. However, we do see near-term pressure from NOR, DDR3/4, and
even SLC/MLC NAND, in all of which  industry supply is ramping faster than demand is
increasing, especially as global cloud capex may face partial delay from 2028 to 2029. We
believe the stock still has strong valuation support at 1.0x P/B, and therefore we believe

an EW rating is fair.

Nanya Tech: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target from NT$600 to NT$550
Our price target (base case scenario value) reflects a target BVPS multiple of 2.04x for
2027 (was 4.47x) versus ROE of 59% for 2027.  We lower our target multiple to reflect
potential peak in DDR4 pricing power and recent memory segment volatility. For Nanya
Tech, our new base case target P/B multiple of 2.04x P/B is stronger than our downside

valuation anchor of 1.0x P/B for capital-intensive IDM/foundry stocks.  This is because we
expect Nanya to remain relatively resilient compared to other DDR4 players in our
coverage, given stronger process technology and potential DDR5 migration.
Our base case value/price target cut of 8% reflects both target multiple reduction and
BVPS estimate adjustment of 3% arising from earnings estimate revisions.
We cut our bull and bear case values from NT$1,270 and NT$330 to NT$1,150 and NT

$300, respectively -- 4.27x and 1.12x 2027e BVPS.
We remain OW
Despite potentially peaking pricing power seen in DDR4, we remain positive on DDR5 in
view of continued strong demand from servers. Nanya is already producing DDR5 on its
second-generation 10-nm process, with DDR5 contributing ~10% of revenue; its next
process enters customer qualification in 1Q27, and EUV implementation is targeted for
2028. This establishes its edge in process technology vs the other DDR4 vendors in our

coverage and a stronger position in DDR5 migration. Valuation is also attractive, in our
view. Therefore, we remain OW on Nanya Tech.

AP Memory: Estimate revisions summary
We  adjust our EPS estimates  +21% for 2026, +2% for 2027, and -12% for 2028: We
factor in 3Q26 preliminary results. We adjust our 2028 forecasts  mainly to account for
supply chain risks. We note that the company's IoTRAM is less affected by  volatility of
typical commodity DRAM pricing. The 2027e and 2028e EPS changes mainly reflect
supply chain uncertainties around S-SiCap. We now reduce our S-SiCap unit shipment

estimates throughout 2027-2028 and  50%+ into 4Q28. We also slightly reduced  our IoT
RAM ASP estimates throughout 2027-28 to reflect peaking DDR3/4 pricing power. In
addition, we reduced our GM assumptions across product lines to reflect potential  peak in
pricing power and supply chain uncertainties. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates
are largely a function of changes in our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is
a good time to make these changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating

price hikes, and potential delay in 2028 US cloud capex.

Phison: Estimate Revisions
We adjust our 2026, 2027, and 2028 EPS estimates: We factor in 3Q26 results, which
demonstrated continued revenue growth (+27% Q/Q) but also gross margin decline
(59.7% in 3Q, down from 65.3% in 2Q). In 4Q26 and 2027, we expect emerging weakness
from consumer tech and a more moderate pricing environment to result in 4Q revenue
decline, with GM starting to normalize. We think normalized GM is likely to extend into

2028.
We present further discussion of our EPS estimates under Valuation Methodology.

Phison: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target from NT$2,588 to NT$1,357
Our price target is our base case value, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Our cut
reflects our latest earnings estimates. We also cut our terminal growth rate assumption
from 4.0% to 3.0% to be broadly in line with a conservative estimate of long-term
nominal economic growth in developed economies. We believe this is appropriate for

Phison because rising storage demand should support continued growth, while declining
NAND prices per bit, competition, and maturing SSD penetration should limit the
conversion of volume growth into earnings growth, bringing its growth closer to that of
the broader economy over time.
Our other RI parameters are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 9.0%, a medium-
term growth rate of 9.0%, and a dividend payout ratio assumption of 50.0%.
Apart from the earnings estimate revisions and terminal growth rate change, a material

part of the price target change is driven by an error in the previously published residual
income model (August 2026). The error has now been corrected. In the previous RI model,
the 2028e net income was mistakenly calculated as 2027e net income * (1+ intermediate
growth rate), where it should have been linked to the actual 2028e net income forecast.
This overstated the previous price target.
Our earnings estimates have also changed materially  from the previously published

version: they increase 12% for 2026, 60% for 2027, and 37% for  2028. This mainly reflects
higher GM assumptions of 61.2%, 51.9%, and 42.0%, respectively; previously we assumed
60.1%, 43.4%, and 39.5%.
Our base case value/price target is lowered 48%, of which roughly +37% is contributed by
earnings estimate revisions, -6% is contributed by terminal growth rate reduction, and -
83% is contributed by the correction of the prior RI model error. (The overall price target

change is calculated from the fully revised RI model. The individual contribution estimates
are illustrative and should not be added together or compounded to derive the overall
change.)
We cut our bull and bear case values from NT$3,400 and NT$1,250 to NT$1,782 and NT
$655, respectively, implying 8.4x 2027e EPS in the bull case and 3.1x 2027e EPS in the
bear case.
We downgrade Phison to UW
The company hopes its AiDaptive module and service business can pick up and offset the

margin pressure in the conventional module business in the coming quarters, which we
believe is worth monitoring. However, its 3Q GM  of 59% missed the Street estimate of
60.3%; we see this as a potential sign that the previously strong margin profile supported
by low-cost inventory may start to face more downside. Specifically, the AI storage part of
its business (eSSD, and cSSD for AI PC, together 40% of revenue) may face margin
decline. This is what we have been cautious about, and we see the 3Q margin miss as

having provided a good opportunity to downgrade this stock, which we view as

overvalued based on our revised assumptions.

to module houses to prioritize their other

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Date: 2026-11-11

### Page 1

Summary: Merida posted its 3Q26 results on 11 November after market hours. Merida’s 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%). Its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower than our 
forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65). Merida plans to hold an 
investor call on 17 November and we will provide an update then.

We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on this company, please 
refer to our latest flash note, 2Q26 review: mainly dragged by Specialized, on 14 August.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results missed. Merida’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD6,098m (-12.1% QoQ, -15.6% 
YoY) missed our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates by 13.6% and 11.1%, 
respectively, which we attributed to the discount when destocking the older models. Its 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%), due to less OBM sales and the price 
discounting for further inventory digestion. Its operating profit of TWD384m (-49.8% 
QoQ, -9.7% YoY) also missed our and the consensus estimates by 35.2% and 37.3%, 
respectively. Its operating margin came in at 6.3% (vs. 11.0% in 2Q26; 5.9% in 3Q25), 
missing our estimate of 8.4% and the consensus of 8.9%. Considering the equity 
investment loss of TWD150m+, its net profit of TWD176m (-44.8% QoQ, -71.9% YoY) 
missed our and the consensus by 65.0% and 65.6%. Its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower 
than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65).

• 4Q26 outlook. We forecast its 4Q26 revenue to decline QoQ as the company will 
continue to manage its existing models and expects new models in 2027 to bring 
stronger demand.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. The stock is currently trading at PERs of 
13.3x/10.7x, based on our 2026/27E EPS, vs. its past-5-year one-year-forward PER trading 
range of 7-33x. Key downside risks: slower-than-expected e-bike penetration in the EU and 
the US, worse-than-expected demand in an inflationary environment and inventory digestion.

2026 年 11 月 2 日 全 6 頁

7-9 月期ユーロ圏 GDP 減速しつつも上振れ 
実質 GDP 成長率は前期比＋0.4％と市場予想を上回る

ロンドンリサーチセンター シニアエコノミスト 橋本 政彦

[要約] 
 ユーロ圏の 2026 年 7-9 月期の実質 GDP 成長率（速報値）は前期比＋0.4％と、4-6 月期
（同＋0.6％）から成長ペースが鈍化しつつも、市場予想（Bloomberg 調査：同＋0.2％）
を上回る底堅い結果であった。 
 ユーロ圏の成長率の半分は、多国籍大企業の影響を受けたアイルランドの高成長によ
るものである点は割り引いて見る必要がある。ただし、アイルランドを除いてもプラ
ス成長を維持しており、エネルギー高を背景としたインフレ率の加速や、記録的猛暑
による悪影響という逆風にもかかわらず、ユーロ圏経済は底堅く推移したという評価
に変わりはない。 
 国別の内訳では、スペインが内需の堅調さを主因として前期比＋0.6％と高成長を維持
し、ユーロ圏全体をけん引する構図が続いた。また、ドイツは同＋0.2％と、前期から
成長率は鈍化したものの、市場予想（同＋0.1％）を上回った。他方、フランスは同▲
0.1％と 2 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長に転じる低調な結果であった。 
 ユーロ圏経済は 10-12 月期も緩やかな景気拡大が続く見通しである。エネルギー価格
の上昇によるインフレ率の加速が個人消費を下振れさせるリスクには注意が必要だが、
ドイツを中心とした拡張財政がユーロ圏経済を下支えすると見込まれる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 6

成長の半分はアイルランドの上振れ、ただしそれを割り引いても底堅い 
ユーロ圏の 2026 年 7-9 月期の実質 GDP 成長率（速報値）は前期比＋0.4％と、4-6 月期（同
＋0.6％）から成長ペースが鈍化しつつも、市場予想（Bloomberg 調査、以下同：同＋0.2％）
を上回る底堅い結果であった。 
ただし、7-9 月期の上振れには、毎期の振れが非常に大きいアイルランドの高成長が影響し
ている点は割り引いて見る必要がある。アイルランドの成長率は前期比＋5.5％と、成長率が
公表された 12 ヵ国の中で突出して高く、これがユーロ圏全体の成長のおよそ半分を占めてい
る。CSO（アイルランド中央統計局）は、多国籍企業の影響が大きいセクターが押し上げ要因
になったと報告しており、一部の多国籍大企業の企業内取引などが成長率を押し上げたとみら
れる。アイルランドを除くユーロ圏 20 ヵ国の GDP 成長率（大和総研試算）は同＋0.2％となり、
実態としての景気回復ペースはヘッドラインよりも緩やかだったとみるべきだろう。もっとも、
アイルランドを除いてもプラス成長を維持しており、エネルギー高を背景としたインフレ率の
加速や、記録的猛暑による悪影響という逆風にもかかわらず、ユーロ圏経済は底堅く推移した
という評価に変わりはない。 
国ごとの成長率を確認すると、スペイン、オランダ、エストニアがいずれも前期比＋0.6％
となり、既述のアイルランドの次に成長率が高かった。スペインの成長率は前期（同＋0.7％）
から減速したが、ユーロ圏全体に対する寄与度はアイルランドに次いで大きく、スペインの高
成長がユーロ圏全体をけん引する構図は 7-9 月期も続いた。また、ドイツは同＋0.2％と、こち
らも前期（同＋0.3％）から成長ペースが鈍化しつつも、市場予想（同＋0.1％）を上回り、ユ
ーロ圏全体の成長を下支えした。他方、不調だったのがフランスであり、増加を見込んでいた
市場予想（同＋0.1％）に反して、同▲0.1％と 2 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長に転じている。

イルランド）を差し引いて計算。

### Page 3

1

3 / 6

フランスは輸出・投資が減少、スペインは引き続き内需主導の成長維持 
ユーロ圏全体の 7-9 月期の需要項目別の内訳は速報段階で明らかになっていないため、以下
では内訳が明らかになっているフランスとスペインの需要項目別の動きを見ていく。 
フランスの実質 GDP 成長率は前期比▲0.1％と、2 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。内・
外需別では、内需の前期比寄与度は＋0.5％pt とプラスだった一方、外需寄与度が同▲0.6％pt
となり GDP を押し下げた。内需の内訳では、在庫変動が最大の押し上げ要因（前期比寄与度：
＋0.4％pt）となった。ただし、内需から在庫を除いた国内最終需要も前期からわずかに増加
ペースが加速しており、GDP 全体としてはマイナス成長ながら、内需については一定の底堅さ
が見られたと評価できる。 
需要項目別の詳細を見ていくと、個人消費は前期比＋0.4％と前期から加速し、これが内需
を押し上げた。増加幅は 2024 年 7-9 月期以来の大きさであり、個人消費に限れば 7-9 月期はむ
しろ好調だったと評価できる。内訳では、財（同＋0.4％）、サービス（同＋0.4％）がいずれ
も前期から加速しており、個人消費は幅広く増加している。財消費では、エネルギーが同＋
1.2％と増加に転じたことが主な押し上げ要因となった。ガソリン価格が上昇する中、石油精
製品の減少は続いたものの、電力消費の増加がエネルギー全体を押し上げた。また、エネルギ
ー以外の財では、電気自動車の販売増加により、輸送用機器が同＋3.1％と好調だった。サー
ビス消費では、娯楽関連を中心とした家庭向けサービスの増加（同＋0.9％）、および宿泊・飲
食サービス（同＋0.7％）が主な押し上げ要因となった。 
好調だった個人消費とは対照的に、総固定資本形成は前期比▲0.6％と 3 四半期連続で減少
し、内需の足を引っ張った。最大の押し下げ要因となったのは、輸送用機器が同▲7.0％と大
幅に減少したことである。統計公表元の INSEE（フランス国立統計経済研究所）の報告によれ
ば、前期にあったクルーズ船の受け渡しの反動、および企業による投資の減少が押し下げ要因
となった。このほか、建設投資が同▲0.5％と 3 四半期連続で減少したほか、不動産取引の停滞
により、法律サービスなどのビジネスサービス（同▲1.6％ ）、 不動産サービス（同▲0.7％）
が前期から減少した。他方、IT 機器の増加により資本財が同＋0.8％と増加したほか、サービ
ス投資の中でも情報通信サービスは同＋2.1％と増加した。 
外需に関して、輸出は前期比▲1.7％と 2 四半期ぶりに減少した。前期に大幅に増加した航空
関連が反動で同▲9.1％と大きく減少したことが減少の主因となった。また、農産品（同▲
7.0％）、農作物加工品（同▲1.1％）が減少したほか、資本財が同▲1.1％と 7 四半期ぶりの減
少に転じるなど、財輸出が総じて振るわなかった。サービス輸出は同＋0.5％と増加を維持し
たが、増加幅は前期（同＋1.0％）から減速している。 
輸入は前期比＋0.1％と、小幅ながら増加した。自動車、および海軍装備品の増加によって
輸送用機器が同＋0.3％増加したことや、資本財の増加（同＋1.4％）が押し上げに寄与した。
他方、石油精製品（同▲6.4％ ）、 農作物加工品（同▲1.6％）が減少したことで、輸入全体の
増加幅は小幅にとどまった。

### Page 4

1

4 / 6

図表2 フランス・スペインの実質GDP 成長率と需要項目別寄与度

（出所）INSEE、INE、Haver Analytics より大和総研作成

スペインの実質 GDP 成長率は前期比＋0.6％となり、4-6 月期（同＋0.7％）から成長ペース
が減速しつつも、高い成長率を維持した。内・外需別では、内需の前期比寄与度が＋0.5％pt、
外需が同＋0.1％pt となり、引き続き堅調な内需が成長のドライバーとなっている。 
個人消費は前期比＋0.6％となった。形態別の内訳では、サービスが同＋0.9％と、15 四半期
連続で増加し好調さを維持したことに加え、耐久財が同＋0.5％と 2 四半期連続で増加し、非耐
久財は同＋0.6％と 4 四半期ぶりの増加に転じている。他方、半耐久財は同▲2.2％と減少に転
じた。 
総固定資本形成は前期比＋0.7％と、11 四半期連続の増加を維持した。ただし、増加幅は
2025 年 4-6 月期以来の小ささとなり、増加ペースは鈍化している。建設投資が同＋1.0％と堅
調さを維持する一方で、輸送用機械の減少が下押し要因となり、機械・設備・武器システムは
同＋0.2％と小幅な増加にとどまった。 
外需に関して、輸出は前期比＋1.3％と 4 四半期連続で増加し、増加幅が前期から拡大した。
インバウンド（非居住者による支出）の増加（同＋1.6％）を中心に、サービス輸出が同＋
1.5％と増加し、輸出全体をけん引する状況が続いているが、サービス輸出の伸びは前期から
縮小した。他方、財輸出は同＋1.1％と、増加幅はサービス輸出に比べて小さいものの、前期
から増加ペースが加速している。 
輸入は前期比＋1.1％となった。サービス輸入が同＋2.1％と大きく増加したことに加え、財
輸入も同＋0.8％と増加した。輸入の増加幅は前期から縮小し、輸出の増加幅を下回ったため、
外需（純輸出）の寄与度は 2 四半期ぶりのプラスに転じている。 
なお、ドイツ（前期比＋0.2％）については、まだ内訳の詳細な計数は公表されていないが、

### Page 5

1

5 / 6

ドイツ連邦統計局からは、消費支出の増加が押し上げに寄与したこと、一方で総固定資本形成
が減少したことが報告されている。また、輸出については好調だった 2026 年上期から減速した
とコメントされた。イタリア（同横ばい）については、内需寄与度のプラスと、外需寄与度の
マイナスがイタリア国家統計局によって報告されている。これらの情報を総合すると、7-9 月
期は、個人消費を中心とした内需がユーロ圏の成長に寄与した可能性が高い。

防衛・インフラ投資が景気拡大を下支え、リスクはエネルギー価格 
ユーロ圏経済の 10-12 月期に入ってからの景気動向に関して、欧州委員会が公表するユーロ
圏景況感指数（総合）を確認すると、2026 年 10 月は前月差▲0.6pt と 2 ヵ月連続で低下した。
もっとも、過去の関係に照らせば 10 月の指数の水準は、景気悪化を示すほどの低さではなく、
ユーロ圏経済は引き続き緩やかなペースでの拡大が続いていることを示唆する。 
景況感指数の内訳を見ると、消費者信頼感指数が前月差▲1.1pt と 2 ヵ月連続で低下したほ
か、これに平仄を合わせる形で小売業が同▲1.5pt と低下した。これは、エネルギー価格の上
昇によってインフレ率のさらなる加速に対する懸念が高まっていることや、生活不安を理由と
した各地での抗議活動の広がりが影響したとみられる。実際、10 月に入って大規模なデモや暴
動が発生したフランスの消費者信頼感指数は、同▲3.4pt と主要国の中でも目立って大きく前
月から悪化した。サービス業の景況感は依然として高水準ながら、同▲0.6pt と 6 ヵ月ぶりの
低下に転じたのも、消費者マインドの悪化や、それを背景とした需要の減少が下押し要因とな
った可能性が高い。

図表3 ユーロ圏の景況感指数と実質GDP 成長率（左）、景況感指数の内訳（右）

（出所）Eurostat、欧州委員会より大和総研作成

一方、家計部門の弱含みとは対照的に、鉱工業の景況感はこのところ堅調さを増している。
鉱工業の景況感指数は、前月差＋0.5pt と 5 ヵ月連続で改善し、2023 年 4 月以来の高さとなっ
た。鉱工業の景況感の回復には、拡張財政による防衛支出・インフラ投資関連の需要の増加が

6 / 6

寄与しているとみられる。足元では長期金利が上昇ペースを速めており、これが設備投資や公
共投資の拡大を抑制する恐れはあるものの、積極的に財政を拡張させるドイツを中心とした防
衛支出、インフラ投資がユーロ圏経済を下支えする構図は当面続くと見込まれる。 
先行きのリスク要因としては、引き続きエネルギー価格の動向、およびその影響の広がりが
焦点となる。今回の 7-9 月期の GDP 統計では、インフレ率が加速する中でも個人消費が底堅く
推移したことが確認された。だが、原油やガスなどのエネルギー価格は上昇が続いており、実
質所得や消費者マインドへの下押し圧力は、今後さらに強まる可能性がある。とりわけ冬季の
需要期にはエネルギー価格上昇による負担感が一層強まるとみられ、個人消費の下振れに警戒
する必要があるだろう。

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000

현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

Document 239: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% YoY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다. 
목표주가 (하향) 2,000,000원  
현재가 (10/6) 1,310,000원

동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중

Document 291: CEE & CCA week ahead_ A rate decision in Hungary and regional price data

Source PDF: all report pdf/CEE & CCA week ahead_ A rate decision in Hungary and regional price data.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Hungary will announce its latest interest rate decision and
release September wage data next week. Meanwhile, October's
core inﬂation data is due from Poland, alongside producer price
ﬁgures from the Czech Republic

Adam Antoniak, Peter Virovacz and David Havrlant
Budapest, Hungary
Poland: Core inﬂation expected to have
eased below 3% in October
Monday brings the release of October's core inﬂation data in
Poland. On the basis of the available data, we estimate that core
inﬂation excluding food and energy moderated below 3% year-on-
year for the ﬁrst time since March. Despite the surge in fuel prices,
there are few signals of broader inﬂationary pressure in Poland's
economy, even though headline inﬂation is elevated. Still, the
National Bank of Poland is sending hawkish signals and may
tighten monetary policy at the beginning of 2027. We expect rates
to be hiked by 50bp in 1Q27.

### Page 2

Hungary: Market sentiment expected to
keep NBH in wait-and-see mode
Tuesday brings the National Bank of Hungary's latest interest rate
decision. While much of the world is grappling with renewed
inﬂation fears, Hungary remains something of an island of safety,
with both headline and core inﬂation hovering around 2% year-on-
year. That gives the NBH room to ease – but the door remains
locked, and global market sentiment holds the key. With markets

increasingly pricing aggressive rate hikes from major central banks
and market tensions elevated, the NBH has little choice but to
remain in wait-and-see mode. The risk is that by the time the
external backdrop structurally improves, domestic inﬂationary
pressures may already have started to close the window for cuts.
For now, low inﬂation argues for easing, but global markets are
forcing patience. In our base case, we see the base rate remaining

at 5.50% for at least the next three quarters.
Wage data for September will be released on Wednesday. Wage
growth will likely remain elevated for now, but the days of
exceptionally strong real wage growth are numbered. Employers
and employees are negotiating a new minimum wage deal for
2027, as the existing three-year agreement would imply an
unsustainably high 14% increase. We expect the new deal to deliver
a rise of around 5%, setting the stage for a meaningful slowdown in

overall wage growth and easing one of the key sources of domestic
inﬂationary pressure.
Czech Republic: Producer prices expected
to have climbed in October
Czech PPI data will be released on Monday. Producer prices likely
continued their monthly gains in October, which have driven the
annual growth pace higher. Both the surging Brent crude price and
depreciating koruna contributed to rising input costs that were only
partially passed to end prices, putting proﬁtability under extended

pressure.

Document 335: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP.  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

8. Across the English Channel, we expect the MPC to raise Bank Rate by 25bp to 4% on

Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 6

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP, and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP, although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research

Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

Document 379: SpaceX与特斯拉世纪合并谈判提速，最快12月宣布交易

Source PDF: all report pdf/SpaceX与特斯拉世纪合并谈判提速，最快12月宣布交易.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

特斯拉定于 12 ⽉ 3 ⽇召开年度股东⼤会，这⼀时间节点与最快 12 ⽉宣布交易的时间线⾼度
重合。⽬前，双⽅正在讨论的核⼼议题之⼀，是 SpaceX 在交易中需向特斯拉投资者发⾏的
股份数量。与此同时，双⽅也讨论了公告推迟⾄ 2027 年初的备选⽅案。
摘要：
SpaceX 与特斯拉的合并谈判正在加速推进，这笔交易⼀旦落地，将成为全球有史以来规模最⼤
的并购案，彻底重塑全球交易市场的边界。
据彭博报道，知情⼈⼠透露，双⽅最快可能在 12 ⽉宣布这笔交易，这⼀激进时间表折射出⻢斯
克在企业运作上⼀贯的快节奏⻛格。与此同时，双⽅也讨论了公告推迟⾄ 2027 年初的备选⽅
案。消息传出后，特斯拉股价当⽇盘中最⾼涨幅达 1.9% ， SpaceX 股价同期上涨 0.5% 。
两家公司的董事会及顾问⽬前正就合并条款、合并后实体的治理架构等核⼼议题展开磋商。然
⽽，交易在正式落地前仍⾯临多重障碍，包括如何隔离特斯拉在中国的业务，以及 SpaceX 涉及
美国政府和国防合同所引发的国家安全敏感性问题。

谈判进展与时间节点

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
据彭博此前报道， SpaceX 与特斯拉此前的⽬标是在明年上半年宣布交易，并⼒争在特朗普总统
任期结束前完成合并。
⻢斯克于 10 ⽉披露，两家公司的董事会已分别成⽴由独⽴董事组成的特别委员会，专⻔负责评
估合并事宜。⽬前，双⽅正在讨论的核⼼议题之⼀，是 SpaceX 在交易中需向特斯拉投资者发⾏
的股份数量。
值得关注的是，特斯拉定于 12 ⽉ 3 ⽇召开年度股东⼤会，这⼀时间节点与最快 12 ⽉宣布交易的时
间线⾼度重合。
两家公司此前已存在业务协同基础 —— 双⽅正联合在德克萨斯州建设⼀座名为 "Terafab" 的半导
体⼯⼚，并持续推进联合运营的前期布局。
监管与政治⻛险构成关键障碍
尽管谈判提速，交易⾯临的障碍不容忽视。据报道，知情⼈⼠指出，如何隔离特斯拉在中国的
业务，是双⽅必须解决的核⼼难题之⼀。

摩根⼤通分析师 Rajat Gupta 上⽉在研报中向客⼾指出， SpaceX 涉及美国政府及国防合同的国家
安全敏感性，与特斯拉在中国规模庞⼤且盈利丰厚的制造业务，构成 " 相互对⽴的关键动态 " ，将
对交易获得监管及股东批准形成重⼤挑战。
与此同时，此次合并也将成为美国中期选举后政治⽣态的重要试⾦⽯。⺠主党在此次中期选举
中重新掌控国会，并已承诺对⻢斯克的商业及政治活动展开调查，这为交易的推进增添了额外
的政治不确定性。
交易规模史⽆前例， AI 战略逻辑驱动合并
此次合并将在华尔街掀起轩然⼤波。合并后的实体将囊括电动汽⻋、储能电池、⽕箭及卫星业
务，合并体量按当前市值估算超过 3.4 万亿美元——SpaceX 市值逾 1.9 万亿美元，特斯拉市值约
1.5 万亿美元。
将上述资产整合于同⼀屋檐下的核⼼逻辑，在于双⽅共同的战略雄⼼：跨越太空、⽆⼈驾驶汽
⻋与⼈形机器⼈三⼤领域，全⾯布局⼈⼯智能。
SpaceX 正式名称为 Space Exploration Technologies Corp. ，总部位于得克萨斯州星际基地

（ Starbase ）。该公司于今年 6 ⽉在纽约完成具有⾥程碑意义的⾸次公开募股，此次上市也令⻢
斯克成为全球⾸位⾝家突破万亿美元的富豪。近年来， SpaceX 已从领先的⽕箭制造商和发射服
务商，转型为向 Anthropic PBC 等企业提供 AI 基础设施的关键供应商，同时也是全球顶级的太空
互联⽹服务提供商。

購與產能承諾達 2,790 億美元，CoWoS 約九成由台積電在台灣封裝。新任

板之產能預訂與規格升級亦可能放慢，影響最快在 2028 年顯現。CoWoS

事件
創辦⼈在董事會調查與國會壓⼒下辭職，財務⻑暫代，正式⼈選由外部顧問協助遴選
兩週內由「董事會⽀持」轉為辭職：先是外國情報⾏動之報導，後是與屬下關係之報導與國會壓⼒
10 ⽉ 29 ⽇（美東時間，下同）媒體報導美國情報評估，⼀名晶⽚買家之主管把資訊傳回北京、並與⿈仁勳關係密切；11
⽉ 4 ⽇眾議院三位委員會主席致函邀其出席閉⾨簡報，11 ⽉ 6 ⽇媒體再報導政府備忘錄認定該⼈為中國情報⼈員。11 ⽉ 8
⽇董事會宣布由獨⽴董事委員會調查，表⽰沒有理由相信國家安全或輝達機密遭到危害、仍信任其領導；⿈仁勳同⽇致信
員⼯承認對婚姻不忠。11 ⽉ 10 ⽇獨⽴董事委任前紐約南區代理聯邦檢察官主持調查；同⽇有媒體報導相關公司網絡協助
⾄少 75 億美元之輝達晶⽚流⼊中國，此數字未經其他媒體證實，輝達先前亦稱相關轉運指控已查明不實。11 ⽉ 11 ⽇兩黨
議員陸續要求其辭職，晚間媒體報導其與⼀位直屬⾼階主管之關係（輝達未回應），董事會當晚開會並接受辭呈，11 ⽉ 12
⽇上午（台北時間 21:52）以 8-K 公告。

治理結構同步調整：⾸度設⽴董事⻑，暫代執⾏⻑兼任財務⻑
⿈仁勳辭去總裁、執⾏⻑與董事，公司發⾔⼈表⽰其⽇後不以任何形式參與輝達。財務⻑ Colette Kress（2013 年加⼊，
曾任 Cisco 與 Microsoft 事業部財務主管）暫代執⾏⻑並續任財務⻑；1993 年起擔任董事、曾任 Synopsys 執⾏⻑之
Harvey C. Jones 出任輝達⾸任董事⻑（輝達過去不設董事⻑）。董事會將考慮內外部⼈選並委請外部公司協助，未公布時
程。第三季財報維持 11 ⽉ 17 ⽇公布；據眾議院外交委員會主席表⽰，⿈仁勳同意於感恩節前赴國會作證。

2027 年以前
2027 年之出貨⼤致鎖定：產品已量產、產能已預付、承諾已簽訂
Vera Rubin 已量產出貨，主要出貨量在 2027 年；Rubin Ultra 以前之規格已定案
輝達 8 ⽉表⽰ Vera Rubin 於當⽉開始量產出貨，管理層預估約占第三季資料中⼼營收兩成；鴻海表⽰ Vera Rubin 第四季
放量、為明年主⼒，廣達表⽰第四季⼩量、2027 年第⼀季放量。管理層 8 ⽉預估 2028 會計年度（約當 2027 年 2 ⽉⾄
2028 年 1 ⽉）營收成⻑約七成，屬供給受限之數字；3 ⽉ GTC 時稱 Blackwell 與 Vera Rubin ⾄ 2027 年之訂單能⾒度⾄少

1 兆美元。據供應鏈訪查，Rubin Ultra 與 Feynman 之開發已進⼊執⾏階段；新任管理層第⼀個能實質調整的，是
Feynman 世代之規格、代⼯與封裝分配。
供應商受合約保護：2,790 億美元之採購與產能承諾，加上客⼾之預付款
輝達 7 ⽉底之季報揭露表外之供應與產能採購承諾 2,790 億美元（前⼀季 1,190 億美元），主要為記憶體與製造設施，台積
電、HBM 廠與主要組裝廠 2027 年之產能多已被預訂。下游⽅⾯，廣達 11 ⽉ 11 ⽇法說會表⽰，迄今未⾒客⼾調整訂單，
客⼾若延後拉貨需負擔相關利息與費⽤；以 CoreWeave 為例，其積壓訂單約 1,325 億美元，未來三個⽉另有約 90 億美元
之客⼾預付款與押⾦，綁定的是機房與晶⽚。
近期之⼲擾來⾃轉換期與出⼝管制，⽽⾮需求
據供應鏈訪查，第四季 VR200、Trainium 3 與 TPU v8 之新平台轉換均因零組件與組裝良率⽽遞延，屬時程問題。較需留
意的是出⼝管制：據報美國商務部正敲定新規，把⾺來西亞、新加坡等 7 國納⼊較嚴格之限制，涵蓋 Blackwell 以上晶⽚

及遠端使⽤；和碩 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇法說會表⽰此類限制並⾮新規，已納⼊法務審核。國會亦把晶⽚追蹤（Chip Security Act）
與出⼝許可審查（AI Overwatch Act）列為國防授權法之候選條⽂。本次事件涉及外國情報與晶⽚流向之指控，調查結果
出爐前，管制收緊之機率已上升，台灣組裝廠出貨東南亞機房之合規成本將提⾼。
接班情境
三種接班路徑對產品節奏、供應商議價與客⼾融資之影響不同；ToAlpha 以內部技術主
管接任為基本情境
董事會之⾸要任務是穩定客⼾與⼯程團隊，內部⼈接任之機率最⾼
據報導與預測市場，內部⼈選以 CUDA 創始⼈、現負責⼤型雲端與 HPC 業務之 Ian Buck 呼聲最⾼，其次為暫代之
Kress；技術⻑ Michael Kagan、⾸席科學家 Bill Dally 與 GPU ⼯程主管 Jonah Alben 亦被提及。外部⼈選包括曾任職輝
3 / 12

### Page 4

達七年之 Arm 執⾏⻑ Rene Haas；Apple 硬體主管 Johny Srouji 據報亦在考慮之列，美國總統則據報公開推薦前 Apple
執⾏⻑ Tim Cook。全球業務主管 Jay Puri 已於 6 ⽉宣布退休。輝達沒有公開之接班計畫、約 60 位主管直接向⿈仁勳報
告；正式⼈選產⽣前由 Kress 暫代，市場預期約⼀年。ToAlpha 以內部技術主管接任為基本情境（45%），營運財務型（含
Kress 續任或外部營運型）30%，外部產業執⾏⻑ 25%。
無論何⼈接任，新增融資與規格升級之積極程度都可能低於⿈仁勳時代
輝達 7 ⽉底持有⾮上市股權約 512 億美元、上市股權約 428 億美元，另有最⾼ 1,085 億美元之保證與 250 億美元之股權投
資承諾；運算承諾⽅⾯，OpenAI 規劃約 12GW 之輝達運算、Anthropic 最多 1GW。已簽承諾會延續，但新增部分之審核
標準可能提⾼：據報 Kress 曾反對為 OpenAI 提供最⾼ 1,050 億美元之擔保；市場亦期待新任管理層提⾼現⾦回饋、回應

「循環融資」之疑慮。規格升級⽅⾯，據供應鏈訪查，⿈仁勳之產品策略對供應鏈有「產能預訂」與「規格升級」兩項激
進影響，我們認為這兩項也是輝達供應鏈過去享有較⾼評價之重要原因。新任管理層若偏重成本與執⾏穩定，800V
HVDC、共同封裝光學、M9 等級銅箔基板等升級之導⼊可能放慢，供應鏈成本管理亦會更嚴格。

資料來源：ToAlpha。⼈選為媒體報導與預測市場所列，⾮公司公布；機率為 ToAlpha 主觀判斷，將隨董事會遴選進度調整。
產品路線
Feynman 以前之路線圖已進⼊執⾏，新任管理層可調整之處在決策速度與規格升級
路線圖本⾝未變，但 2026 年已出現取捨：Rubin CPX 擱置、Kyber 機櫃傳延⾄ 2028 年
Rubin 為兩顆光罩極限尺⼨晶粒、每顆搭載 288GB HBM4 之設計[5]，量產爬坡已受 HBM4 規格與良率牽制。輝達今年並
把原訂 2026 年底之 Rubin CPX 移出路線圖，改以 Groq 授權技術之 LPX 推論機櫃處理解碼；產業研究機構 7 ⽉指出
Kyber 機櫃因 PCB 中背板之可製造性延⾄ 2028 年，輝達回應路線圖不變。這顯⽰即使在⿈仁勳任內，年更節奏已觸及供

應鏈之物理極限。我們認為新任管理層較可能之調整，是把年更改為「⼤世代兩年、中間做強化版」，並集中資源於推論
（LPX、Vera CPU）與網路（Spectrum-X、共同封裝光學）。
開放策略可能加速：NVLink Fusion 讓 ASIC 客⼾也採購輝達元件
NVLink Fusion 已納⼊ AWS Trainium4、Marvell 客製 XPU 與 Intel 客製 x86 CPU，輝達 3 ⽉並⼊股 Marvell 20 億美元。
對新任管理層⽽⾔，與其與 ASIC 正⾯競爭，不如在 ASIC 機櫃內銷售 CPU、網卡、交換器與 NVLink；輝達之營收來源因
此分散，但 GPU 本⾝之份額讓渡也會更快。對台灣供應鏈⽽⾔，交換器、網卡相關供應商不必選邊。

AMD∕博通∕ASIC
Google TPU 與 AMD 最直接受益，CoWoS 份額之移轉在事件前已開始
事件前之路徑：輝達占 CoWoS 需求由 2026 年 56% 降⾄ 2027 年 45%
據供應鏈訪查，2027 年全球 CoWoS 需求約 2,694 千⽚，輝達 1,222 千⽚、成⻑ 57%，⾮輝達合計成⻑ 140%；AMD 因
OpenAI、Meta 與 Anthropic 之 MI450 系列放量，份額由 9% 升⾄ 20%，聯發科（Google TPU）由 3% 升⾄ 7%。產業研
究機構亦預期 TPU 出貨 2027 年倍增以上，⾼於輝達⾼階 GPU 約三成之成⻑。換⾔之，輝達之份額下滑早於醜聞；接班不

確定性讓既有趨勢加速，⽽⾮創造新趨勢。
Google TPU 最直接：客⼾與供應鏈之信任轉移最快
據供應鏈訪查，在輝達⾯臨接班不確定性下，Google TPU 最可能先取得客⼾與供應鏈之進⼀步信任，設計夥伴博通與聯發
科最直接受惠。博通 9 ⽉表⽰ AI 營收 2026 會計年度約 580 億美元、2027 年約 1,150 億、2028 年約 2,300 億美元，六家
XPU 客⼾中 Anthropic 2027 年部署 5GW、2028 年再 10GW，OpenAI ⾃研推論晶⽚ 2027 年 1.3GW、2028 年逾 5GW。聯
發科表⽰第⼆顆 ASIC 按計畫於 2028 年量產。
AMD：具名承諾最多 14GW，2027 年起才⼤量部署，能否接住移轉取決於 Helios 機櫃之量產速度
AMD 第三季資料中⼼營收 85.5 億美元、年增 97%，MI455X 已開始量產出貨，具名承諾 OpenAI 6GW、Meta 6GW、
Anthropic 2GW。據供應鏈訪查，Helios 機櫃之⼤量出貨在 2027 年第⼆季。AMD 之 MI350 把 3 奈⽶運算晶粒 3D 堆疊於

6 奈⽶ I/O 晶粒之上[4]，MI450 系列據報再採 2 奈⽶運算晶粒、每顆 432GB HBM4，以單晶⽚規格⾒⻑，但機櫃級整合之
經驗仍少於輝達。輝達若在 2027 年邊際讓出份額，AMD 之瓶頸是 CoWoS 與 HBM 配額，⽽⾮客⼾意願。
⾦額份額與顆數份額差距⼤：ASIC 顆數 2028 年超越 GPU，⾦額仍以輝達為主
產業研究機構估計客製 ASIC 出貨顆數將於 2028 年超越資料中⼼ GPU；以⾦額計，據供應鏈訪查 ASIC 占加速器市場由約
⼀成升⾄ 2027 年約兩成。輝達 Vera Rubin 每 GW 營收約 400 億美元，博通估其每 GW 內容價值約 250 億美元（其估計之
中值）。份額移轉對輝達營收之影響因此⼩於對出貨顆數之影響；但對依顆數與機櫃數計價之台灣組裝與零組件供應商，影
響較接近顆數。
技術差距縮⼩：AMD 與 ASIC 之單晶⽚規格追近，輝達之優勢轉向機櫃整合與軟體
以廠商公布之稠密峰值計，AMD MI455X 之 FP4 約 40 PFLOPS、Rubin 約 35 PFLOPS[2]（輝達官⽅標⽰之 50 PFLOPS 為

NVFP4 推論值）；Google TPU 歷經五代維持架構穩定，以規模與能效取勝[1]。輝達之差異化愈來愈落在 NVLink 機櫃整
合、網路與 CUDA ⽣態，⽽這些正是需要領導⼈跨部⾨拍板之領域；接班空窗期之⾵險，在於競爭者單晶⽚規格追近之
際，輝達之系統整合節奏放慢。

### Page 6

ToAlpha 份額移轉之推導：融資收斂與客⼾分散下單，2027 年約 2、2028 年約 4 個百分點
2027 年：明年約四分之⼀業務需輝達融資⽀撐，若新增融資縮減⼀⾄⼆成，約為輝達量 2.5%〜5%，以 45% 份額換算約
1〜2 個百分點，加上客⼾邊際分散下單，取 2 個百分點（1.5〜2.5）。2028 年：融資依賴之需求縮減約兩成（約輝達量
5%），加上⼤型客⼾新專案約 4% 轉向 AMD 與 ASIC，合計約輝達量 9%、約 4 個百分點（3〜5）。輝達減少之量，2027 年
七成、2028 年⼋成由 AMD 與 ASIC 依原份額吸收，其餘為依賴輝達融資、隨之消失之需求，CoWoS 總量因此少約 0.6%、
0.8%。供應商營收以平台出貨量⽐例估算，未區分每⽚晶圓之價值差異。

之⽐重為 ToAlpha 依法說會說法取整之假設，個別公司可能與組別平均差異明顯；台積電與⽇⽉光投控以 CoWoS 總量計。影響為 AI 營收，⾮淨利。
韓國∕中國∕美國
台灣份額 2027 年前難以外移；韓國之⾓⾊在⿈仁勳任內已擴⼤，美國是⽣產地點之轉
移，中國沒有承接能⼒
物理限制：輝達之 CoWoS 約九成在台灣封裝，美國之 CoWoS 級產能最早 2028 年初
據供應鏈訪查，輝達 2027 年約 1,222 千⽚之 CoWoS 需求中，約 132 千⽚由 Amkor 承接，其餘由台積電封裝。美國⽅

⾯，Amkor 亞利桑那廠 2028 年初量產、台積電亞利桑那封裝廠尚無 CoWoS 量產時程、SK 海⼒⼠印第安那 HBM 封裝廠
6 / 12

### Page 7

2029 年、三星泰勒⼆廠 2030 年；亞利桑那⽣產之 Blackwell 晶圓⽬前仍須運回台灣封裝。因此 2027 年以前之 Rubin 與
Rubin Ultra，前段晶圓與封裝⾼度依賴台灣；新任管理層第⼀個可調整之世代是 Feynman。
韓國：HBM 主要在韓國，增量在封裝與代⼯，且多為⿈仁勳任內之決定
據業界估計，Rubin 之 HBM4 由 SK 海⼒⼠供應約三分之⼆、三星約四分之⼀以上；產業研究機構 6 ⽉⼤幅上修三星之
HBM4 份額，三星今年亦發表單堆疊 36GB、3.3TB/s 之 HBM4[3]。台灣在 HBM 之⾓⾊主要為美光在台產能。韓國之增量
有兩處：推論晶⽚ LP30 交由三星 4 奈⽶；據報輝達 10 ⽉宣布與 Amkor ⾄ 2028 年底把韓國中介層產能提⾼ 300% 以上。
另據報輝達要求記憶體廠把客製 HBM 之 base die 交台積電代⼯，則對台灣有利。新任管理層若偏重成本與供應分散，韓
國在⾮旗艦產品之代⼯與封裝份額可能再提⾼，但旗艦 GPU 之前段 2028 年前仍無替代。

中國：管制只會收緊，台廠之⾵險在出貨中國客⼾與東南亞機房
輝達財測沿⽤不計⼊中國資料中⼼運算營收之做法；產業研究機構估計 2026 年上半年輝達在中國 AI 加速器之份額降⾄
34%、華為升⾄ 28%。本次事件涉及晶⽚流向中國之指控，新任管理層之合規只會更嚴，供應鏈不會流向中國；受影響的
是出貨中國客⼾或東南亞機房之台灣組裝廠，例如英業達 5 ⽉法說會表⽰其 AI 伺服器以中國客⼾占⽐偏⾼。
美國：地點轉移由台廠執⾏，營收仍在台灣公司，但成本較⾼
美國商務部⻑表⽰⽬標把台灣約四成半導體產能移⾄美國；輝達宣⽰在美國⽣產最多 5,000 億美元之 AI 基礎設施，緯創德
州廠已⽣產 GB300 並準備 Vera Rubin，鴻海休⼠頓廠興建中、規劃⽣產 GB300 運算模組。新任執⾏⻑在國安疑慮之政治
環境下，加速「美國製造」之誘因更⾼；但承接者仍是台積電、鴻海、緯創、廣達等台廠，屬地點轉移。美國本⼟之替代
以 Intel 與 Amkor 為主：據報 Feynman 之 I/O 晶粒與最多四分之⼀之最終封裝可能交 Intel，據供應鏈訪查 Google TPU

後續世代預計採 Intel EMIB-T 封裝；這類分散化之決定權在新任管理層⼿上。

美國、墨西哥廠由台廠經營 地點轉移、營收不轉移；⽑利率承壓
電源、散熱、機構
件
台廠為主 美國廠多在興建 規格升級節奏放慢之影響⼤於份額
資料來源：輝達、台積電、Amkor、SK 海⼒⼠、三星、產業研究機構、據供應鏈訪查；ToAlpha 整理。
直接供應鏈
量之衝擊⼩於規格升級節奏之衝擊：2027 年之價值成⻑相當部分來⾃ Vera Rubin 之規
格
機櫃數之估計分歧，單櫃價值翻倍是共同看法
據供應鏈訪查，NVL72 機櫃出貨 2026 年約 82 千櫃、2027 年約 90 千櫃；產業研究機構則估計 2027 年成⻑逾五成，兩者
差距⼤。共同之看法是 Vera Rubin 系統平均售價約為 GB300 之兩倍，廣達亦表⽰新世代 GPU 伺服器單價⾼四到五成。
7 / 12

### Page 8

2027 年輝達供應鏈之成⻑相當部分來⾃單櫃價值，⽽單櫃價值來⾃規格升級，這正是新任管理層最可能放慢之部分。組裝
份額⽅⾯，鴻海由 2026 年約 37% 升⾄ 2027 年約 43%，廣達約 26%，緯創約 24%。
受影響之先後：規格升級受惠者最先，依機櫃數計價者次之，晶圓與封裝最後
800V HVDC 電源（台達電、光寶科）、共同封裝光學、M9 等級銅箔基板（台光電、聯茂）、液冷與雙⾯冷板（奇鋐、雙鴻）
等受惠於規格升級之供應商，其時點已隨 Kyber 傳延⾄ 2028 年；更替使「升級是否如期」之不確定性增加。依機櫃數計
價之組裝、滑軌、機構件次之。台積電與 CoWoS 因產能不⾜、可轉給 AMD 與 ASIC，受影響最⼩。另據供應鏈訪查，台灣
供應商將⾯臨更嚴格之價格談判，訂單亦可能進⼀步分散；新任管理層若以成本管理⾒⻑，台灣組裝廠本已偏薄之⽑利率
將承壓。
終端客⼾結構亦重要：neocloud 對輝達融資之依賴⾼於⼤型雲端業者
⼤型雲端業者以⾃有現⾦流與⻑約採購，neocloud 與前沿實驗室則較依賴輝達之⼊股、保底合約與擔保。若新任管理層收

緊新增融資，受影響之需求集中在 neocloud；和碩表⽰其伺服器⽬前以 neocloud 與⽩牌為主。同樣以輝達平台為主，客
⼾以⼤型雲端業者為主者（如廣達）較為穩定。
台股曝險
以輝達平台為主之台股：2027 年影響有限，不利處在缺少 ASIC 動能與 2028 年之份額
移轉
市場⽬前只區分出 ASIC：以 ASIC 為主者相對抗跌，純輝達與兩邊都有者差異不⼤
10 ⽉ 29 ⽇⾄ 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇，加權指數 −7.1%，32 家中以 ASIC 為主之 10 家平均 −9.4%、以輝達平台為主之 5 家 −14.0%、
兩邊都有之 17 家 −17.0%；11 ⽉ 12 ⽇傳出董事會準備撤換之當⽇，三組分別 −5.2%、−5.3%、−2.8%（加權 −2.6%）。此
期間同時有美國期中選舉後之科技股修正，兩邊都有之組別多為前波漲幅⼤之零組件股，跌幅亦⼤，個股差異不全來⾃本
事件。市場對純輝達者尚未額外折價，反映 2027 年之訂單能⾒度。
純輝達之台股：2027 年營收有保護，不利處在缺少 ASIC 動能與 2028 年之份額移轉

以輝達平台為主之五家，2027 年之 Vera Rubin 出貨已排定，單價提⾼四到五成，營收成⻑仍可期；ToAlpha 基本情境之
份額移轉僅使其 2027 年 AI 營收相對原本路徑少約 3.4%。不利之處有三：其⼀，缺少 2027〜2028 年成⻑最快之 ASIC 動
能；其⼆，2028 年份額移轉使其 AI 營收少約 6.7%，若規格升級放慢，單櫃價值之成⻑亦打折；其三，依賴輝達之配貨與
融資，新任管理層之成本與融資政策直接影響其⽑利與客⼾。廣達已表⽰⾮ GPU 平台 2027 年將升⾄雙位數、和碩有 4〜5
家 ASIC 客⼾將於 2027 年放量，組裝廠可逐步降低集中度；我們認為華碩、技嘉之伺服器業務較難轉向 ASIC，因雲端業者
⾃研晶⽚多直接委託 ODM。華碩、技嘉、和碩之⾮ AI 業務占⽐亦⾼，股價受 AI 以外因素影響⼤。緯創⺟公司以輝達運算
板為主，惟合併⼦公司緯穎後屬兩邊都有。
兩邊都有之供應商：份額移轉之影響約 −1%，⾵險在規格升級節奏
奇鋐表⽰「GPU 與 ASIC ⾮⼆選⼀、兩邊都出貨」；據供應鏈訪查，雙鴻之 ASIC 營收 2027 年可望約占四成。這類供應商在

份額移轉下之 AI 營收相對原本路徑少約 1.3%（2027 年）、2.3%（2028 年），其⾵險在規格升級之節奏，⽽⾮平台選擇。
台積電與⽇⽉光投控依 CoWoS 總量計，影響約 −0.6%、−0.8%。

觀察指標
未來三個⽉之五個觀察點，決定份額移轉落在情境區間之哪⼀端
11 ⽉ 17 ⽇第三季財報：暫代執⾏⻑是否重申 2028 會計年度約七成之成⻑、Vera Rubin 之放量節奏，以及回購與新增
融資之態度。
正式執⾏⻑⼈選：內部技術主管、營運財務型或外部產業執⾏⻑，對應圖表 3 之三種情境。
供應鏈之產能預訂與規格定案：台積電 2027 年 CoWoS 分配、HBM4E 與 Rubin Ultra 之規格、800V HVDC 與共同封裝

光學之量產時點有無延後。
出⼝管制：⿈仁勳赴國會作證後，晶⽚追蹤與出⼝審查法案能否併⼊國防授權法、東南亞 7 國限制是否成案，以及對伺
服器出貨之實際影響。
客⼾之分散下單：⼤型雲端業者與 neocloud 是否把邊際訂單轉向 AMD 與 ASIC，以及 AMD Helios 之量產速度。
⾵險
本報告之主要不確定性在於接班⼈選、調查結果與 CoWoS 產能是否仍不⾜
接班⼈選與調查結果：董事會調查與國會調查若發現更多出⼝管制問題，影響可能擴及輝達之營運許可，份額移轉將超
出本報告區間；反之若迅速確定具技術背景之接班⼈，2028 年之移轉可能⼩於基本情境。
產能與吸收假設：本報告假設 CoWoS 與 HBM 2027〜2028 年仍不⾜、輝達減少之量七⾄⼋成由 AMD 與 ASIC 吸收。若
產能轉為過剩或依賴融資之需求消失更多，份額移轉將伴隨總量下降，對台積電與兩邊都有之供應商亦不利。
分組與⽐重：各公司之輝達⽐重為依法說會說法之 ToAlpha 判斷，部分公司未揭露客⼾結構；供應商營收以平台出貨量

⽐例估算，未區分每⽚晶圓之價值。個別公司之實際影響可能與所屬分組平均有明顯差異。
股價區間：事件期間之股價差異同時受美國期中選舉後之科技股修正與個別公司消息影響，不宜全數歸因於本事件。

重要聲明
本報告由 ToAlpha（toalpha.tw）研究部編製，屬純產業研究，⾮投資建議。本報告僅描述產業、供應鏈與情境影響，不對任何個股提供評等、⽬標
價或買進、賣出、持有之建議，亦不構成任何證券之要約或要約之引誘。報告中之份額變動、營收影響與情境機率係 ToAlpha 依公開資料、供應鏈訪
查與⾃訂假設推算，涉及多項假設，實際結果可能與本報告所述有重⼤差異。本報告所引⽤之資料雖來⾃我們認為可靠之來源，惟 ToAlpha 不保證其
正確性與完整性，資料與觀點如有變動恕不另⾏通知。讀者應⾃⾏判斷並承擔投資⾵險，ToAlpha 對任何⼈依本報告所為之決定不負任何責任。未經

Document 411: US Midterms_ The cost of living is now a pivotal issue (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Midterms_ The cost of living is now a pivotal issue (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026EDITORIAL
US MIDTERMS: THE COST OF LIVING IS NOW A PIVOTAL ISSUE
1 AP-NORC, October 2026.
2 Jared Bernstein, Daniel Posthumus, ‘The way we were: Price-level shocks and consumers’ memories’, SIEPR, May 2026.
3 72% of registered voters rank ending the war with Iran as one of the top priorities for the next Congress, including 60% of Republicans (AP-NORC, October 2026).

4 Oleg Korenok, David Munro, Jiayi Chen, ‘Inflation and attention thresholds’, GLO Discussion Paper Series, No. 1175, Global Labour Organization, 2022.
5 Rajashri Chakrabarti, Thu Pham, Beck Pierce, Maxim L. Pinkovskiy, ‘Tracking the K-shaped economy: who’s driving spending?’, Federal Reserve Bank of New York, Liberty
Street Economics, 1st May 2026.
6 The year 2020 is excluded due to the disruptions caused by lockdowns.
In the United States, growth is holding up, unemployment remains low and consumer spending shows no sign of slowing.

On paper, that should flatter the president’s party ahead of the midterms. Yet, Republicans could lose control of Congress.
The rise in the cost of living, which weighs on households far more than macroeconomic aggregates suggest, could be one
of the reasons. On top of persistent inflation, rekindled by the war in Iran, there is also a slowdown in wage growth and
rising housing costs. The election could hinge on affordability, yet whichever party wins will lack the means to address it.

A change in the majority in Congress will nonetheless have an impact on the country’s economy.
On 3 rd November, Americans will elect a new Congress for the next
two years. With Republicans currently in control of the White House,
the House of Representatives and the Senate, the balance of power in
Washington could shift. Polls suggest that the House, at least, could
swing to the Democrats. Beyond the new balance of power, the vote
will reflect how voters view the way the country is being run. Here, we

focus on the cost of living.
INFLATION: THE INCUMBENTS’ BURDEN
The incumbent party goes into the election with two handicaps.
The first is structural: no matter who holds the White House, the pre -
sident’s party almost always loses seats in midterm elections. The
second stems from the combination of robust but uneven growth
and persistent inflation, which is undermining household confidence.
Pre-election polls1 confirm this assessment: the economy and the cost-

of-living top voters’ worries.
Inflation has overshot the Fed’s target every year since 2021. Prices are
up 28% over five years: 31% for housing (not including mortgage rates,
which have more than doubled), and 29% for food. This shock is felt all
the more keenly as it is the most severe in forty years and follows on
from decades of low inflation2. Furthermore, it hits the lowest-income
households the hardest. In addition, the election comes after an ener-

gy-driven rise in inflation. Energy accounts for the entire gap between
the latest CPI reading (3.7% y/y in September) and the last figure re -
corded before the conflict in the Middle East started (2.4% y/y).
Fuel has made things worse. The rise in the price of a gallon (46%
between February and October) caused by the Iran war — which is
unpopular even amongst Republican voters3 — is an aggravating factor.
In 2026, inflation only slightly crossed the ‘attention threshold’, the

3.6%y/y rate beyond which households start to regard it as problema -
tic4. Fuel is a recurring, unavoidable expense whose price is directly
observable, unlike many items in the CPI basket. This results in a subs-
tantial gap between perceived inflation and measured inflation.
Inflation must be viewed alongside the other drivers of households’
well-being. Today, wages, payrolls and the housing market are moving
the wrong way for most Americans.
IT'S NOT JUST PRICES

The 2022 midterms suggest caution in reading the polls. Democrats
defied expectations of a ‘Republican wave’, retaining control of the Se-
nate and losing the House by just a few seats, even though inflation
averaged 8% that year. This reminds us that inflation must be viewed
alongside the other drivers of households’ well-being. Indeed, how
people experience the cost of living depends on the interplay between
prices, wages and housing. In 2022, wages, payrolls and the housing

market were all growing faster than in 2026. Today, all three are mo -
ving the wrong way for most Americans.
Wage growth is slowing just as inflation picks up again. In September
2026, average hourly earnings were up 3% y/y in nominal terms, the
slowest rate since 2018, and now below inflation. Real wage earnings
are therefore falling (-0.7% y/y in September, with six out of nine mon-
ths showing a decline in 2026). The rise in the cost of living is thus

compounded by a net loss of purchasing power. The Atlanta Fed reports
that wage growth is lower for the bottom quartile of earners (the 25%
of employees with the lowest pay) than for the top quartile (the 25%
with the highest pay): 3.8% y/y vs. 4.4% on average since late 2024,
following 10 years in which the gap favoured the bottom quartile. Top
earners also benefit most from the wealth effect linked to stock mar -
ket valuations. This explains the resilience of aggregate consumption.

However, this rests on a relatively narrow base, mostly households
earning more than USD 125,000/year5. Meanwhile, a falling saving rate
(3.8% in September 2026, down 1.4pp y/y) suggests households are
drawing on their savings to offset price rises. A large share of the po -
pulation is therefore excluded from the growth that has been observed.
Beyond wages, there is the question of employment prospects.
The unemployment rate remains low and consistent with full employ-

ment. Yet the labour market looks stagnant: low firing despite repeated
shocks and low hiring despite solid growth. Payroll growth (an average
of 68,000 per month since January) is well below that of the previous
decade (nearly 200,000 per month between 2011 and 2019 6). Com -
bined with a workforce that is no longer growing due to an ageing
population and a restrictive immigration policy, this is enough to keep
the unemployment rate low and the labour market looking healthy.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
But households are likely to be suffering from a lack of opportunities,
and consequently from a lack of prospects for wage growth that would
help them to increase their purchasing power. The Conference Board’s
labour differential, a gauge of perceived job availability, says as much:
outside the pandemic, it is at its lowest since 2015.
Housing is the area that has deteriorated the most. The rise in

mortgage rates that began in 2022, from under 4% to over 7%, has
dampened the property market. This is true on the demand side, as
expected, but also on the supply side. The sharp rise in interest rates
is deterring homeowners with low-interest mortgages from selling, as
they would have to borrow again at current rates. This ‘lock-in effect’
prevents prices from falling despite weaker demand. The lock-in effect
is also affecting tenants, as the decline in the availability of housing is

driving up rents. Housing lies at the heart of the disconnect between
the macroeconomic picture and voters’ perception of it, as they face a
multifaceted rise in the cost of living. That rise is also driven by credit:
monthly repayments on a home of the same value have risen shar -
ply. This additional cost, which inflation figures do not capture7, erodes
households’ purchasing power in the property market.
A CONGRESS POWERLESS ON THE COST OF LIVING

The next Congress will, in all likelihood, have little room for ma -
noeuvre. Neither party is within reach of the thresholds required to
override the filibuster (which allows 41 out of 100 senators to block
a bill from coming to a vote) and the presidential veto (two-thirds of
both chambers).
Affordability is likely to be a key issue in the election, but the winners
will lack the tools to address it. The Democrats’ agenda relies notably
on the reintroduction of schemes that were abolished or scaled back

under the current administration (Affordable Care Act tax credits, Me -
dicaid). Even if Congress passed them, they would be subject to a pre-
sidential veto. There is no question of direct support for households or
a restrictive fiscal policy.
Whether inflation falls will depend on two factors: how the conflict in
Iran unfolds and the effectiveness of monetary policy. The labour mar-
ket should hold its current balance: robust growth (see our EcoFlash)

is supporting demand, whilst failing to alleviate supply-side pressures,
and the unemployment rate could continue to fall. Household senti -
ment will continue to depend on how prices develop. As for housing,
it is unlikely to improve in the short term. With the 10-year Treasury
yield still at 4.8% at the end of 2027 (BNP Paribas forecast), 30-year
mortgage rates would, at best, remain around 7%.
THE OTHER ECONOMIC STAKES OF THE VOTE
Historically, divided governments have brought legislative stability,

which is viewed favourably by the markets. But polarisation has in -
creased in recent years and is likely to feature prominently in the bud-
get vote and the debt-ceiling debate. The debt limit, in particular, could
be reached as early as Q1 2027, depending on the Treasury’s borrowing
requirements. The current administration, meanwhile, has taken major
decisions (on tariffs, immigration and Iran) without involving Congress,
a practice that is likely to continue.

7 Marijn A. Bolhuis, Judd N. L. Cramer, Karl Oskar Schulz, Lawrence H. Summers, ‘The cost of money is part of the cost of living: new evidence on the consumer sentiment
anomaly’, NBER Working Paper, No. 32163, 2024.
Risks to the deficit remain tilted to the upside. According to our base -
line scenario, it is expected to remain stable at 6.3% of GDP in 2027,
but the post-election balance of power could add to the cost of the war
in Iran and rising debt-service costs. In particular, interests could align

in favour of increased spending should the Democrats gain control of
both houses of Congress. Avoiding, or ending, a shutdown could require
a trade-off involving mutual concessions from the presidency on key,
popular aspects of the Democratic agenda (such as healthcare), and
from the Democrats on defence.
The AI sector is exposed to the vote. The economic stakes are high
given how much the associated investment cycle and the wealth effect
contribute to US growth. However, at this stage, the risk is not legisla-

tive. As with the cost of living, sweeping legislation is unlikely, all the
more so with a president holding a pro-AI stance, and who holds the
right of veto. The risk lies rather in sentiment and further out. A Demo-
cratic sweep could lead to increased congressional activism on regu -
lation, on concentration in the AI industry, and on the knock-on effects
on the cost of living (data centres and electricity) and on jobs. Putting
these issues on the agenda could create a climate conducive to tighter

regulation in the medium term and weigh on the sector’s valuations.
Furthermore, the mid-term elections will see 36 governors’ seats up
for election. These governors have extensive powers (regarding autho-
risations and incentives) when it comes to the establishment of data
centres in their states.
Finally, the midterms will matter for monetary policy. The Senate
confirms, by simple majority, the president’s nominees to the Fed’s
Board of Governors. Consequently, should the Senate come under De -

mocratic control, President Trump would need a consensus pick to re-
place Governor Powell from 2028 — or to fill any other seat that might
become vacant during the next Congress.
*****
Whatever the outcome of the election, disinflation and, more broadly,
relief from the cost of living, depend less on Congress than on the
White House and, to a lesser extent, the Fed. The key factor lies in the
resolution of the conflict in Iran, coupled with an effective monetary

policy to enable a sustainable return to price stability.
Anis Bensaidani
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

Document 421: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA- SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.
